Wait, why is h1g1, h1f1, h1e1 legal? Because it's a rook.
Why is d1e1, d1c1 legal? King is on d1.
Why is h2h3, h2h4 legal? Pawn on h2.

Is there any way to save the f6 pawn? The black rook on f3 attacks it.
Neither the King on d1 nor the Rook on h1 can protect f6 in one move.
The pawn on f6 will likely fall.

Let's consider the king move d1e1.
Or the rook move h1f1.
If h1f1, the rook moves to f1. Now the pawn on f6 is defended by the rook on f1!
Yes! That's it. h1f1 defends the pawn at f6.

If White doesn't defend f6, Black will play ...Rxf6.
By playing h1f1, White protects the f6 pawn and prevents the rook at f3 from taking it.

Let's check the other moves.
h1g1 doesn't defend f6.
h1e1 doesn't defend f6.
h2h3/h4 doesn't defend f6.
d1e1/c1 doesn't defend f6.

So the most logical move is h1f1.
